2027 Forensic FOI Programme

The Project Office · Mandate · Master plan · Highlight reports · Dartboard source · That Old Chestnut source

2027 Forensic FOI, Records Recovery and Institutional Accountability Programme

  • Programme PRG-2027-FOI
  • Subprojects 88 institution / recipient lanes
  • Delivery 2027 · four delivery stages
  • Status DRAFT BASELINE
  • Tasks 1,408 measurable tasks

A forensic repeat of the 2026 disclosure campaign, rebuilt around a verified question register, records-custody mapping, measurable work packages, next-working-day response discipline, independent QA and evidence-led accountability. The programme covers the wider Great House Farm / Tŷ Mawr / Llandough-juxta-Penarth / Leckwith landscape, not one parcel or one institution.

Baseline qualification: the Dartboard tracker generated 24 November 2026 reports 39 institutions, 260 threads, 138 statutory requests, 400 emails sent, 365 replies and 200 substantive replies. Its main mailbox cut-off is 18 October; targeted HMLR and National Archives additions extend to 24 November. Request-status data is principally dated 21 October with targeted additions to 24 November. HR-06 is 22 October; HR-07 was still marked OPEN in the 30 October master report. These are provisional baselines, not a fully reconciled year-end count. Refresh before approving the 2027 baseline.

1 · Programme mandate and success criteria

What 2027 must deliver

Purpose: identify, obtain, preserve, compare and accurately describe records that can answer the historical questions; ensure every institutional response is assessed against the exact question and record sought; and pursue proportionate procedural accountability where a documented failure warrants it.

  • Every 2026 request, response, attachment, draft, internal review, complaint and follow-up is reconciled to one canonical record.
  • Every outstanding question has a unique QID, source, institution, owner, answer test, next action and evidence-based disposition.
  • Each of the 88 institution/recipient subprojects has a record map, question matrix, request sequence, deadline controls and closure report.
  • Every incoming email is triaged by the next working day; routine outgoing drafts are prepared and independently reviewed within one working day or have a recorded exception.
  • Every due date is independently calculated and monitored; no reply is checked on the next working day and chased where appropriate.
  • Every received attachment is read and reconciled before a response is marked substantive.
  • Every complaint is routed to the correct body and confined to matters within that body's jurisdiction; no allegation is made without evidence.
  • Every unresolved question ends the year answered, partially answered with reasons, confirmed not held after adequate search, transferred, lawfully withheld with review route recorded, or explicitly unresolved with the next viable route.

Non-goals: this programme does not pre-judge motive, institutional conduct, title entitlement or legal error. It is a research and disclosure programme, not litigation. The conditional legal reopening gate remains separate and dormant unless the required evidence and independent legal advice threshold is met.

Mandatory lessons translated into controls

  • Our own response latency caused avoidable drift: every inbound message gets triaged by the next working day; substantive draft/review target is one working day, with exceptions escalated.
  • Follow-up was not systematic: next-working-day acknowledgement checks, automated due-date calculations, pre-due reminders and first-working-day-overdue chases become mandatory.
  • A single mailbox thread was not enough: cross-thread and cross-reference matching must be done before concluding that an answer is absent.
  • The tracker coverage was not fully current: publish an as-of date, coverage boundary, refresh log and reconciliation status with every metric.
  • Attachments and PDFs were sometimes received but not read: an acknowledgement or attachment receipt is never a substantive answer until every attachment is opened and indexed.
  • Requests were sometimes too broad or poorly sequenced: map records, holders, regimes, aliases and question dependencies before drafting; split independent record families where helpful.
  • A body may answer one point while leaving others open: use numbered question-by-question answer matrices, not a single replied/unreplied status.
  • Institutional delays and requester's own delays must be separately measured; do not attribute our delay to the authority or vice versa.
  • No reply, late reply, changed wording or not-held statement is a lead for verification, not proof of dishonesty or misconduct.
  • 2026 highlight reports showed the value of per-body markers, legal due-date checks and pre-meeting action sweeps, but also revealed review capacity bottlenecks, stale snapshots, inconsistent denominators and uncompleted tasks.
  • No work packages were formally authorised before 19 October 2026; the 2027 PID, Project Board, tolerances, stage plan and work-package authority must exist before delivery begins.
  • January–September reports recorded no Project Board and no agreed time, cost, quality, scope, benefits, risk or sustainability tolerances; establish these at the 2027 initiation gate.
  • Monthly reports repeatedly recorded no completed products; measure accepted products and evidence outcomes, not correspondence volume or activity.
  • The 28 September Wales Office and National Archives internal-review drafts were recorded as not sent; every draft now has an owner, review slot, due date and daily queue check.
  • The National Archives disclosure of 23 September was not yet entered in the evidence library; ingest and index every incoming record within one working day.
  • The National Archives search was widened after an initially narrow search; search by creator, company, litigation, registration, transfer, aliases and record series, not only the property name.
  • Museum Wales site-wide scope risked being narrowed to a single object; preserve the full question scope and record every proposed narrowing with reasons and approval.
  • A register-level conclusion is not proof of the underlying historical title; test it against the first-registration file, complete instruments, plans, objections and transfer records.

3 · PRINCE2 governance, roles and controls

Management by stages and exception

Executive: The Executive retains authority for external action, public release, complaints, spend and legal decisions. AI Project Manager: maintains plan, schedule, dependencies, RAID, reporting and handovers. Senior User: assures the research questions and disclosure products meet their intended purpose. Senior Supplier: assures the AI/research/development outputs and resources. Project Assurance: independent agent(s), not the drafter, test completeness, provenance, fairness and quality.

HubSpot is the private task and review register; GitHub Issues are the public-safe engineering ledger; Dartboard is the disclosure/failure marker; Projects is the programme view; Hive is coordination and handover only. Do not create a competing task database.

ControlRule
CTRL-01Every inbound message is triaged by next working day; acknowledgement, classification, task and next action recorded.
CTRL-02Every request has one owner, one independent reviewer, one statutory/voluntary route, one calculated due date or explicit no-clock status, and one next-action date.
CTRL-03Check for replies on the next working day after a due date; if absent, create a chase task the same day.
CTRL-04No outgoing draft waits unowned: routine draft target 1 working day; complex draft has a named owner, reviewer and dated reason for delay.
CTRL-05A response is not complete until all attachments are read, all numbered questions are dispositioned and cross-thread replies are checked.
CTRL-06Keep FOIA, EIR, SAR, archive enquiry, voluntary private-party request, regulator complaint and ombudsman complaint distinct.
CTRL-07Each request's legal basis, clock, extension, pause, clarification effect and appeal route is independently verified; do not assume one deadline applies to all.
CTRL-08Never label a person a liar. Record the exact statement, date, source, contradiction, alternative explanation and evidence needed to resolve it.
CTRL-09Complaints address evidenced service or procedural failures, not the merits of disputed history unless the route has jurisdiction.
CTRL-10No external email, formal complaint, payment, legal filing or public allegation is sent by an agent without the required human authorisation.

Escalation tolerance: any statutory deadline missed, any unanswered inbound message beyond one working day without triage, any draft idle beyond one working day, any attachment unread beyond two working days, any unowned task, or any unresolved complaint deadline is reported as an exception to the Project Manager immediately and to the Executive in the next daily exception digest. These are internal management tolerances, not a statement of any authority's legal obligations.

4 · Stages and stage gates

Four 2027 stages plus mobilisation
StagePeriodExit criteria
P0 · Mobilise and reconcile2026-11-25 to 2026-12-312026 mailbox, Dartboard, HR-06/HR-07, Old Chestnut and Q&A sources reconciled; approved question register and 2027 baseline; all open 2026 deadlines handed over.
P1 · Forensic records mapping and question design2027 Q1Each institution has verified records map, unresolved-question matrix, request sequence, legal route, retention/preservation map and QA-approved drafts.
P2 · Disclosure and parallel archive recovery2027 Q2All planned requests submitted and tracked; all received responses and attachments fully analysed; alternative holders searched; internal reviews started where justified.
P3 · Escalation and accountability2027 Q3Each unresolved refusal, delay, search issue or service failure has a documented remedy decision and any justified complaint prepared/filed through the correct route.
P4 · Closure, synthesis and year-end review2027 Q4Every question answered or given a documented final disposition; institutional histories reconciled; lessons, year-end report and 2028 options approved.

Each stage-end decision is proceed, amend, pause or stop. No stage is passed merely because tasks were attempted; products must be accepted against their quality criteria.

5 · Programme-wide work packages

Cross-institution controls

GWP-01 · Canonical 2026 source reconciliation

  1. Refresh all 2026 Gmail FOI/EIR/SAR/complaint threads, attachments, drafts and sent items through 31 December.
  2. Reconcile the 260-thread tracker, 261-record request-status register, 318-conversation 6 November count, 84-lane roadmap, 515+ subject-search matches and GHF label search without combining dates or denominators.
  3. Reconcile GHF label metadata (603 all-time), 2026 monthly GHF search lower bound (401+), 11 action-needed threads, 11 awaiting-reply search results (13 in label metadata), 31 evidence/attachment threads and 145 auto-replies; deduplicate by thread ID.
  4. Audit all 19 Gmail drafts: classify 10 active/held, 4 archived/superseded, 1 cancelled, 2 templates, 1 empty placeholder and 1 unrelated; map every active draft to its existing thread and HubSpot review task. Do not send archived or held drafts.
  5. Read all 31 evidence/attachment threads and every attachment; create stable evidence IDs, record missing pages/annexes and link each item to the relevant subproject.
  6. Reconcile HR-06 and HR-07; extract every open action, decision, risk and missed follow-up from the 2026 highlight-report series.

GWP-02 · Q&A unanswered-question closure

  1. Verify the dedicated Q&A repository unclehowell/cedwyn-qa, deployed route, current release/build commit and source coverage.
  2. Reconcile the 362-record Q&A Hall snapshot dated 19 October and the 23 October correspondence snapshot (304 requests, 406 questions, 467 replies) against the current live export; do not treat historical counts as current.
  3. Verify the daily 04:17 UTC refresh workflow and last successful run; restore and validate static/qa/kb.json and static/qa/api/qa.json if the live export is missing or stale.
  4. Export every Hall item and classify answered, partly answered, not held, refused, redirected, pending, no reply, contested, statement only or open; preserve exact wording, source references and stable IDs. The Hall's open-question count remains unknown until this export is recovered.
  5. Import all 20 case questions (5 open, 5 family-account, 4 contested, 4 documented, 2 not-held) and all 7 research questions (5 open, 1 partly answered, 1 answered); review family-account and contested items for independent evidence.
  6. Create one traceable QID task for each of the 10 explicit open questions below, link it to the relevant institution subprojects and source records, and preserve the 2 research follow-ups as separate statuses.
  7. Resolve the discrepancy between the 14 November changelog's six research questions and the current seven-item JSON; retain the 23 November drainage-instruments addition.
  8. Reconcile the Q&A Hall against Gmail and Dartboard; the refresh automation does not read Gmail, HubSpot, Hive or private drafts, so it is not a complete substitute for mailbox review.

GWP-03 · Old Chestnut forensic accountability

  1. GWP-03-T1 — Reconcile all That Old Chestnut entries against complete original requests, full threads, attachments, later mail and actual records; current Peekaboo feed contains six observations including TOC-F01/F02/F03/F05 and two catalogue/metadata observations.
  2. GWP-03-T2 — For each observation, classify exactly: later record located, catalogue corrected, scope disagreement, partial disclosure, delay, or wording-only; record alternative explanations and what would falsify the concern.
  3. GWP-03-T3 — Prepare a complaint eligibility assessment per institution: jurisdiction, procedural prerequisite, limitation, evidence, remedy, proportionality and correct route; draft only where threshold met.

GWP-04 · Question bank and request engineering

  1. GWP-04-T1 — Build master question register with unique QID, source lead, importance, institution, record family, date span, aliases, requested output and answer test.
  2. GWP-04-T2 — Run missing-question analysis against each 2026 request, response, refusal, clarification, internal review and Q&A item.
  3. GWP-04-T3 — Run red-team review: what else could be asked, who else may hold it, what predecessor/successor body existed, what record would disprove the working hypothesis.

GWP-05 · Operational speed and follow-up

  1. Create next-working-day inbox triage and due-date controls with named owner and backup.
  2. Enforce one-working-day routine drafting and review target; report every exception with a reason and recovery date.
  3. Create 5/2/0-day reminders, next-working-day overdue chase, and 5/10-working-day escalation review where proportionate.
  4. Run weekly sweep of sent mail, drafts, HubSpot tasks, statutory clocks, complaints, preservation notices and unanswered QIDs.
  5. Daily review of the 11 action-needed and 11 awaiting-reply search results; resolve the 13-versus-11 awaiting-label discrepancy and record next action for each thread.
  6. Verify the correct current HM Land Registry contact route after three unsupported-email auto-replies; do not resend to a retired mailbox.
  7. Schedule the Mount Stuart Collections Team follow-up for January 2027; the October auto-reply states general external enquiries cannot be answered before then.

GWP-06 · Independent quality and reporting

  1. GWP-06-T1 — Every task has a testable acceptance criterion, source links, evidence date, reviewer and result.
  2. GWP-06-T2 — Weekly dashboard: requests due, on time, overdue, authority delay, requester delay, unanswered question count, attachments unread, drafts waiting, complaints eligible and work packages blocked.
  3. GWP-06-T3 — Monthly PRINCE2 highlight report: RAG, products accepted, tolerance forecast, top risks/issues, decisions needed, stage progress and lessons.

GWP-07 · Records destruction, loss and custody audit

  1. GWP-07-T1 — For every claimed destroyed/lost/not-held record, request disposal schedule, authority, date, method, transfer, incident report, inventory and audit trail where held.
  2. GWP-07-T2 — Search alternate repositories and predecessor bodies; distinguish never-created, destroyed, transferred, miscatalogued, unlocated and withheld.
  3. GWP-07-T3 — Preserve evidence of each version of catalogue descriptions, search statements, correspondence and disclosure decisions.

GWP-08 · Dartboard roadmap and per-recipient ladder audit

  1. Reconcile all 84 unique Dartboard roadmap lane IDs and 924 escalation steps against the 86 subprojects; preserve lane IDs and the original 21 November snapshot.
  2. For each lane, verify every step's legal basis, recipient policy, prerequisite, deadline, source URL and check date; classify statutory, good-practice, discretionary, parallel, unavailable or not applicable.
  3. Retain the recipient-specific ladder; do not substitute one institution's route for another's.

GWP-09 · Exhaustive alternative-options sweep

  1. For every unanswered question, list all plausible record holders, predecessor/successor bodies, archive deposits, contractors, published catalogues and third-party custodians.
  2. Assess FOIA, EIR, SAR, law-enforcement access, court-record access, land-register copies, archive reading room, voluntary enquiry and regulator/ombudsman processes.
  3. Record why every route is used, deferred, unavailable or disproportionate; no route is silently omitted.

GWP-10 · 2026 withdrawals and live carryovers

  1. Preserve the 18 requester-initiated closures from 19 November as a separate status; do not erase earlier clock results or count them as institutional non-response.
  2. Reconcile each live 2026 carryover, including ICO, HMLR, MoJ, NPCC, Museum Wales and preservation notices, before issuing a repeat request.
  3. For each closed/withdrawn lane, decide whether a fresh 2027 request is needed, what gap it answers and how it differs from the withdrawn request.

GWP-11 · PRINCE2 initiation and work-package authority

  1. Approve the 2027 PID, continued business case, Project Board roles, project approach, stage plan and product descriptions before delivery starts.
  2. Set time, cost, quality, scope, benefits, risk and sustainability tolerances; define exception thresholds and decision rights.
  3. Issue a signed work-package instruction for every task group with owner, reviewer, dependencies, acceptance test, evidence outputs and due dates.

GWP-12 · Evidence ingestion and scope integrity

  1. Ingest each incoming disclosure and attachment into the evidence library within one working day with stable evidence ID, source, date, version, page count and checksum where available.
  2. Reconcile all pages and attachments against the response schedule; flag missing pages, unreadable scans, missing annexes and unexplained redactions.
  3. Preserve the full scope of site-wide and multi-record requests; log any narrowing, its reason, the decision-maker and independent QA approval.

GWP-13 · Highlight-report discipline and product acceptance

  1. Produce a weekly highlight report with actuals, forecast, tolerance status, product acceptance, requester response latency, authority delay and unresolved decisions.
  2. Run a daily unsent-draft sweep and a weekly product/evidence ingestion sweep; no draft or disclosure remains unowned.
  3. Do not report an activity as a completed product; record the acceptance test, reviewer, evidence and any outstanding defect.

6 · Institution subprojects

88 separate work breakdown structures

Each institution/recipient lane has four milestones, eight work packages and 16 individually identified tasks. The 84 unique Dartboard lanes are preserved by stable lane ID; two additional recipient/parent lanes and two Q&A-identified archive holders are retained separately. The task descriptions are contextualised to the institution's known record families. All tasks begin NOT_STARTED; a task cannot be marked complete without its output, source references, date, named agent role, independent reviewer and acceptance result.

SP27-02 · Dŵr Cymru Welsh Water

Water utility / EIR and voluntary records

Scope: Historic water supply, mains, service connections, plans, works, property/customer records, archive transfers and the 2026 not-held position.

SP27-02-M0 · 2026 evidence baseline and preservation

[NOT_STARTED · Research agent · QA: Independent QA agent]

  • SP27-02-T015 — Search predecessor bodies, successor bodies, archives, deposited collections, published catalogues, contractors and lawful non-FOI sources for every unresolved record. Institutional focus: Historic water supply, mains, service connections, plans, works, property/customer records, archive transfers and the 2026 not-held position. [NOT_STARTED · Research agent · QA: Independent QA agent]
  • SP27-02-T016 — Close only when every question has an evidenced disposition, all appeal/complaint choices are recorded, preservation is addressed and a reusable lesson is logged. Institutional focus: Historic water supply, mains, service connections, plans, works, property/customer records, archive transfers and the 2026 not-held position. [NOT_STARTED · Research agent · QA: Independent QA agent]

Institutional focus: 26FOI 103, IR/26/16, land ownership and historic farm records, transfers and correspondence with Welsh Government. [NOT_STARTED · Research agent · QA: Independent QA agent]

[NOT_STARTED · Research agent · QA: Independent QA agent]

Institutional focus: FOI-26-46, FOI-26-54, 1965–71 revision, consultation/notification, mapping source records, Red Fen/Cedfin and historical map provenance. [NOT_STARTED · Research agent · QA: Independent QA agent]

Scope: Heritage advice, casework, surveys and correspondence concerning Great House Farm/Tŷ Mawr or related buildings.

SP27-82-M0 · 2026 evidence baseline and preservation

2 work packages · 4 tasks

SP27-82-WP01 · Mailbox and record reconciliation

  • SP27-82-T001 — Retrieve every 2026 thread, attachment, draft, sent item and relevant archive copy; map each to an official reference and this institution. Institutional focus: Heritage advice, casework, surveys and correspondence concerning Great House Farm/Tŷ Mawr or related buildings. [NOT_STARTED · Research agent · QA: Independent QA agent]
  • SP27-82-T002 — Reconcile Dartboard tracker rows against Gmail and WhatDoTheyKnow; resolve duplicate, split, cross-thread and possible-reply-elsewhere cases. Institutional focus: Heritage advice, casework, surveys and correspondence concerning Great House Farm/Tŷ Mawr or related buildings. [NOT_STARTED · Research agent · QA: Independent QA agent]

SP27-82-WP02 · Preservation and source map

  • SP27-82-T003 — Build a dated inventory of record series, departments, systems, predecessor bodies, archive transfers and known retention schedules. Institutional focus: Heritage advice, casework, surveys and correspondence concerning Great House Farm/Tŷ Mawr or related buildings. [NOT_STARTED · Research agent · QA: Independent QA agent]
  • SP27-82-T004 — Record preservation requests, acknowledged holds, known destruction/loss, and the evidence supporting each status. Institutional focus: Heritage advice, casework, surveys and correspondence concerning Great House Farm/Tŷ Mawr or related buildings. [NOT_STARTED · Research agent · QA: Independent QA agent]

SP27-82-M1 · Forensic question design and search specification

2 work packages · 4 tasks

SP27-82-WP03 · Records and custody map

  • SP27-82-T005 — Identify the named business areas, archive locations, search systems, custodians by role, aliases and date ranges relevant to the stated institutional focus. Institutional focus: Heritage advice, casework, surveys and correspondence concerning Great House Farm/Tŷ Mawr or related buildings. [NOT_STARTED · Research agent · QA: Independent QA agent]
  • SP27-82-T006 — Build a record-series matrix: creator, title/description, date span, format, current holder, transfer route, retention/disposal status and alternative holder. Institutional focus: Heritage advice, casework, surveys and correspondence concerning Great House Farm/Tŷ Mawr or related buildings. [NOT_STARTED · Research agent · QA: Independent QA agent]

SP27-82-WP04 · Question matrix and request drafting

  • SP27-82-T007 — Convert every unanswered Q&A item and 2026 gap into a numbered question with source, why it matters, exact record sought and a testable answer format. Institutional focus: Heritage advice, casework, surveys and correspondence concerning Great House Farm/Tŷ Mawr or related buildings. [NOT_STARTED · Research agent · QA: Independent QA agent]
  • SP27-82-T008 — Draft a narrow, searchable request with alternative identifiers, aliases, map/grid context, date bands, schedule fields, advice-and-assistance request and transfer question. Institutional focus: Heritage advice, casework, surveys and correspondence concerning Great House Farm/Tŷ Mawr or related buildings. [NOT_STARTED · Research agent · QA: Independent QA agent]

SP27-82-M2 · 2027 disclosure execution and response analysis

2 work packages · 4 tasks

SP27-82-WP05 · Submission and deadline control

  • SP27-82-T009 — Submit only after scope/legal-route QA; save exact sent copy, delivery proof, official reference, statutory regime and independently calculated due date. Institutional focus: Heritage advice, casework, surveys and correspondence concerning Great House Farm/Tŷ Mawr or related buildings. [NOT_STARTED · Disclosure agent · QA: Independent QA agent]
  • SP27-82-T010 — Check acknowledgement and inbox/archive next working day; chase missing acknowledgement, clarification or overdue response against the recorded clock. Institutional focus: Heritage advice, casework, surveys and correspondence concerning Great House Farm/Tŷ Mawr or related buildings. [NOT_STARTED · Disclosure agent · QA: Independent QA agent]

SP27-82-WP06 · Forensic response and attachment audit

  • SP27-82-T011 — Read every reply and attachment; create a point-by-point answer matrix marking answered, partial, refused, not held, transferred, unclear or unanswered. Institutional focus: Heritage advice, casework, surveys and correspondence concerning Great House Farm/Tŷ Mawr or related buildings. [NOT_STARTED · Disclosure agent · QA: Independent QA agent]
  • SP27-82-T012 — Test search adequacy, locations, aliases, date coverage, exemptions, public-interest reasoning, cost estimates, advice/assistance and consistency with earlier replies. Institutional focus: Heritage advice, casework, surveys and correspondence concerning Great House Farm/Tŷ Mawr or related buildings. [NOT_STARTED · Disclosure agent · QA: Independent QA agent]

SP27-82-M3 · Escalation, alternative routes and closure

2 work packages · 4 tasks

SP27-82-WP07 · Review and accountability route

  • SP27-82-T013 — Prepare a proportionate clarification or internal review identifying each defect, evidence, requested remedy and applicable procedure; do not bundle unrelated routes. Institutional focus: Heritage advice, casework, surveys and correspondence concerning Great House Farm/Tŷ Mawr or related buildings. [NOT_STARTED · Research agent · QA: Independent QA agent]
  • SP27-82-T014 — Assess ICO, regulator, ombudsman, corporate complaint or parliamentary route separately; draft and submit only when jurisdiction, evidence, timing and prerequisite checks pass. Institutional focus: Heritage advice, casework, surveys and correspondence concerning Great House Farm/Tŷ Mawr or related buildings. [NOT_STARTED · Research agent · QA: Independent QA agent]

SP27-82-WP08 · Alternative source, closure and lessons

  • SP27-82-T015 — Search predecessor bodies, successor bodies, archives, deposited collections, published catalogues, contractors and lawful non-FOI sources for every unresolved record. Institutional focus: Heritage advice, casework, surveys and correspondence concerning Great House Farm/Tŷ Mawr or related buildings. [NOT_STARTED · Research agent · QA: Independent QA agent]
  • SP27-82-T016 — Close only when every question has an evidenced disposition, all appeal/complaint choices are recorded, preservation is addressed and a reusable lesson is logged. Institutional focus: Heritage advice, casework, surveys and correspondence concerning Great House Farm/Tŷ Mawr or related buildings. [NOT_STARTED · Research agent · QA: Independent QA agent]

No request is assumed sent by this plan. Task status remains NOT_STARTED until an agent records the source, output and independent QA result.

7 · Q&A unanswered questions and missing-question sweep

Mandatory mobilisation gate

Q&A source located: the dedicated repository is unclehowell/cedwyn-qa. Its changelog validates a 362-record knowledge base dated 19 October 2026; a 23 October deployment snapshot records 304 requests, 406 questions and 467 replies.

Important coverage limitation: the Q&A refresh automation reads the canonical catalogue API and allowlisted read-only public MCP endpoints; it does not read Gmail, HubSpot, Hive, private drafts or the open web. The generated static/qa/kb.json and static/qa/api/qa.json were not present in the repository's default branch during this audit, and the public page crawl showed loading placeholders. The 362-record figure is therefore a historical baseline, not a verified live export. The Hall's current unanswered count remains unknown until the export is restored and enumerated.

Case questions: 20 records — 5 open, 5 family-account, 4 contested, 4 documented and 2 not-held.

Research questions: the current JSON contains 7 records — 5 open, 1 partly answered and 1 answered. The 14 November changelog lists six; the seventh, drainage-instruments question, is dated 23 November. Preserve this version discrepancy.

Explicitly open case questions (5)

  1. cq-bute-acquisition — When and how did the Bute Estate acquire Great House from the Vaughans?

    open
    Bute bought the lordship of the manor in 1793. The heritage record records Bute acquired the freehold of Great House in the early 19th century. The conveyance from the Vaughans or their heirs has not been located, so the date, the price and any leases it recited are unknown.

    Next recorded steps: NLW Bute Estate Records: the Vaughan conveyance and abstract of title; Mount Stuart: Bute estate deeds (research visit)

  2. cq-demolition — Who was involved in the eviction and demolition of 30 November – 6 December 1988?

    open
    Press reports record the eviction on 30 November and demolition from about 4am on 6 December 1988 by BP Properties. South Wales Police reported no records under FOI 719/26 (internal review 1188/26); on FOI 1264/26 (operational records) the force asked on 13 Oct 2026 for the names of individuals or entities involved, and suspended the clock under s.10(6) until clarified. The MOD relied on section 23(5) for the Special Forces element of FOI2026/09019 and reported nothing in scope on the SAR.

    Next recorded steps: SWP FOI 1264/26: send the clarification (BP Properties Ltd, its agents and contractors, the bailiffs, the family) so the clock restarts; MOD internal review DPR2026/46503; RCAHMW: Suggett's post-demolition visit, Archive 6048945

  3. cq-mary-death — Where is Mary Doreen Williams's death registration (1983)?

    open
    FreeBMD indexes a 1983 death. The Vale of Glamorgan and Cardiff register offices both report not holding the registration (30 Sep and 12 Oct 2026); Cardiff has passed the search on.

    Next recorded steps: Cardiff Register Office onward search (13 Oct 2026 reply)

  4. cq-1910 — What does the 1910 Valuation Office survey record for Great House?

    open
    The National Archives identified the Valuation Office map for OS Glamorgan XLVII.6 (12 Oct 2026). The field book (DVAL/1/33 per Glamorgan Archives) would record owner, occupier and hereditament for 1910, which would show whether the house and fields were then valued as one unit or two.

    Next recorded steps: Order the IR 58 / map copy for XLVII.6 and the field book entry

  5. cq-drainage-instruments-1905-1924 — What rights and land were affected by the 1905 Bute–Cardiff Corporation conveyance and the 1924 Bute–Corbett drain agreement?

    open
    The existing title-history catalogue identifies a Bute–Cardiff Corporation conveyance dated 23 February 1905 (EV-0013) and a Bute–James Pelham Stuart Corbett agreement dated 15 May 1924 concerning construction and maintenance of a drain (EV-0015). The register descriptions are leads only: the original deeds and plans have not yet been examined. They do not establish that Cardiff Corporation or Corbett acquired the freehold, occupied a defined site, or obtained title by adverse possession. The 1916 Bute–John Williams yearly tenancy (EV-0014), described in BP Properties Ltd v Buckler [1987] EWCA Civ 2 as covering 'the whole farm', is relevant counter-evidence to any claim that Bute had already disposed of the entire holding.

    Next recorded steps: Obtain the complete 1905 conveyance and all plans; identify whether it granted an easement, conveyed a defined site, or did both; Locate deposited plans and contemporary records for the Western Sewer and any pumping-station compound; plot the route against Great House Farm parcels; Obtain the original 1916 tenancy and plan, if extant, and establish whether any infrastructure site was excluded; Obtain the 15 May 1924 drain agreement and plan; compare its parties, route and land with the 1905 instrument; Identify the 1961 deed and the exact parcel and instrument relied on in the later first-registration process

Explicitly open research questions (5)

  1. which-other-recorded-buildings-stood-within-200-m-of-the-house — Which other recorded buildings stood within 200 m of the house, and do site files survive for them?

    open
    The national record (Coflein) has four entries close to the house besides Great House Farm itself (NPRN 20182). NPRN 15342 is "Llandough, Remains of House At", a medieval house at ST 1682 7320, about 110 m south of the house. NPRN 19886 is Rose Cottage, a later 17th-century house at ST 1674 7320, ruinous when surveyed in 1964 and since demolished, about 130 m to the south-west. NPRN 19159 is Llandough Grange, a post-medieval dwelling at ST 1685 7313, about 185 m to the south-south-east. NPRN 400051 is the Roman villa site at ST 1680 7330, excavated in 1979. The entries for 15342 and 19159 carry no description. Distances are worked from the grid references, which are given to 10 m. Points for: • Coflein NPRN 15342, 19159, 19886 and 400051 (read 13 November 2026) Not yet established: • Whether NPRN 15342 is the same medieval house remains reported south of the church in 1963 and 1979 • Which of these stood on the house parcel, the fields parcel or neither Records still to check: • The National Monuments Record site files behind NPRN 15342, 19159 and 19886 (an enquiry is drafted, not sent) • The tithe map and apportionment, for the plot numbers under each grid reference

    Next recorded steps: No next step recorded; create one after source review.

  2. what-do-the-mathew-family-papers-show-about-llandough-and-leckwith — What do the Mathew family papers show about Llandough and Leckwith holdings before the Bute estate?

    open
    Glamorgan Archives holds the papers of the Mathew family of Radyr and Llandaff (DMAT). The published list runs to DMAT 206–207. It records a Vaughan holding in the manor of Leckwith in 1682, other owners granting land there in 1715, 1729 and 1734, and Mathews "of Llandough" through the 18th century. A later item, DMAT/279, is a late 18th-century plan of "Ty Mawr farm, Llandough [Gedrych property]" (photocopy; catalogue record MED-0208). Points for: • DMAT 168: surrender and admission of Morris Vaughan to a tenement and land in the manor of Leckwith, November 1682 • DMAT 177: Thomas, Viscount Windsor, conveys a cottage and land in the manor of Leckwith to Lewis Morgan of Leckwith, yeoman, 7 and 8 December 1715 • DMAT 170 and 171: a cottage and land near Leckwith church, leased by the Dowager Countess of Pembroke on 23 October 1729; a 99-year lease at the Manor of Leckwith Court and Audit, 5 December 1734 • DMAT 2, 7, 60, 187 and 189: Anthony Mathew of Llandough (will, 1740), William Mathew of Llandough (leases, 1770 and 1774), William John of Llandough, farmer (marriage bond, 1774), John Mathew of Llandough (inventory, 1805) • DMAT 69: copy entries from the Leckwith, Llandough and Michaelston-le-Pit parish registers • DMAT 175–176: William Gedrych of Leckwith, with Leckwith Farm in his occupation, 1829; DMAT 53: Gedrych v. Mathew, 1829 Not yet established: • Which Llandough the Mathew entries mean. DMAT 69 groups Llandough with Leckwith and Michaelston-le-Pit, which points to Llandough near Penarth; the list does not state it • Whether Morris Vaughan of 1682 belongs to the Vaughan family recorded at Great House • Which land the "Gedrych property" plan covers, and where the original plan is held A name to keep apart: "Great House" in these papers is usually the Mathew family's house at Whitchurch, a different place. Records still to check: • The list for DMAT 208 onwards, and the catalogue entry for DMAT/279 (an enquiry is drafted, not sent) • DMAT 168, 171 and 177 themselves

    Next recorded steps: No next step recorded; create one after source review.

  3. do-the-plymouth-estate-plans-show-the-boundary-with-the-great-house-holding — Do the Plymouth (Windsor) estate plans show the boundary with the Great House holding?

    open
    Three plans in the Plymouth estate collection at Glamorgan Archives (DPL) cover Llandough before and at the time of the tithe survey. None has been seen yet. A plan of a neighbouring estate marks its own edge, so these may show where the Windsor and Clive lands stopped and who held the land next to them. Points for: • DPL/6: plan of Lord Plymouth's estates in Penarth, Llandough, Cogan and Lavernock, about 1768 • DPL/7: rough sketch maps, about 1820–1840, including a tracing for Llandough • DPL/8: plan of Lady H. Clive's lands in Cogan and Llandough parishes, about 1840 Not yet established: • Whether any of the three reaches the land beside St Dochdwy's Church • Whether the neighbouring owners and occupiers are named on them Records still to check: • DPL/6, DPL/7 and DPL/8 (an enquiry on copying is drafted, not sent) • The Plymouth rentals and abstract of leases for Glamorgan, 1712–1797, in the same collection

    Next recorded steps: No next step recorded; create one after source review.

  4. where-did-marconi-stay-during-the-may-1897-trials-and-who-was-mr-williams — Where did Marconi stay during the May 1897 trials, and who was the Mr Williams at Lavernock?

    open
    The family-derived account is that Marconi lodged at Great House during the trials and that a Mr Williams of the house helped with a horse and cart. No record made at the time and naming the house has been found yet. A 1993 journal article puts Marconi's headquarters for the trials at the Penarth Hotel, with the trials at Lavernock Point between 6 and 29 May 1897. The Evening Express of 15 May 1897, as transcribed in a 2019 local history article, names "Mr Williams (of the engineering department, Cardiff)" as fixing a 120-foot pole at Lavernock Point. That is a second Williams at the trials, so the two must not be merged. Points for: • The family-derived account, repeated in the 1974 press reports Points against: • BIAS Journal 26 (1993): headquarters at the Penarth Hotel • Post Office Limited, FOI2026/00751: no record held of a Mr Williams and the 1897 experiments Not yet established: • Where Marconi's assistants and the Post Office staff lodged • Whether the Mr Williams of the newspaper report was a Post Office engineer with no link to the house Records still to check: • The Evening Express page of 15 May 1897 itself (Welsh Newspapers Online) • George Kemp's diaries for May 1897 (Marconi Archives, Bodleian Library)

    Next recorded steps: No next step recorded; create one after source review.

  5. what-rights-did-the-1905-and-1924-drainage-instruments-grant-at-great-house-farm — What rights did the 1905 and 1924 drainage instruments grant over land associated with Great House Farm?

    open
    The title-history schedule identifies a Bute–Cardiff Corporation conveyance dated 23 February 1905 (EV-0013) and a Bute–James Pelham Stuart Corbett agreement dated 15 May 1924 for construction and maintenance of a drain (EV-0015). The original instruments and plans have not yet been examined. The register descriptions alone do not establish a freehold transfer, exclusive occupation or adverse possession. The 1916 Bute–John Williams yearly tenancy (EV-0014), described in BP Properties Ltd v Buckler [1987] EWCA Civ 2 as covering 'the whole farm', is relevant counter-evidence to any suggestion that Bute had already disposed of the entire holding.

    Next recorded steps: Obtain the complete 1905 conveyance and plan; establish the legal interest and exact land affected; Plot the Western Sewer and any pumping-station compound against the 1905 and later title plans; Compare with the 1916 tenancy and the 15 May 1924 drain agreement and plan; Identify the 1961 deed and exact first-registration parcel and instrument

Research follow-ups (not counted as open)

  1. what-has-welsh-government-published-about-the-1988-cadw-inspection — What has Welsh Government published about the 1988 Cadw inspection?

    answered
    Welsh Government published its response to request ATISN 27021 on its disclosure log, dated 11 June 2026. It records that Cadw inspectors visited and assessed the house and that it did not meet the criteria for listing. The only record it holds is two inspection photographs, of the house and of the barn, dated 29 July 1988. It holds nothing else for 1970–1999, and records that other papers would have gone to the Royal Commission (RCAHMW) or been destroyed under the Public Records Act 1958. Points for: • Welsh Government disclosure log, ATISN 27021, 11 June 2026 Not yet established: • The inspectors' written assessment, and the date and authority for the disposal of the 1988 file Records still to check: • RCAHMW, for anything received from Cadw or the Welsh Office in 1988–89 • The replies to ATISN 26-0009 and 26-0075, listed in the hall

    Next recorded steps: No next step recorded; create one after source review.

  2. what-did-the-ministry-of-defence-state-about-armed-forces-involvement-in-1988 — What did the Ministry of Defence state about armed forces involvement in 1988?

    partly answered
    The Ministry of Defence replied to request FOI2026/09019 on 29 May 2026, and the reply is published on WhatDoTheyKnow. For Special Forces it relied on section 23(5) of the Freedom of Information Act and would neither confirm nor deny that information is held, adding that this is no indication either way. For the rest of the request (military aid to the civil authorities, and correspondence with the police, the council and the Welsh Office) it stated that no information is held. It pointed to The National Archives at Kew for unit historical records and commanders' diaries of the period. Points for: • Ministry of Defence reply, FOI2026/09019, 29 May 2026 Not yet established: • Which record systems and date ranges were searched for the part reported as not held Records still to check: • The National Archives: unit historical records and commanders' diaries for late 1988 • The internal review of FOI2026/09019, listed in the hall

    Next recorded steps: No next step recorded; create one after source review.

8 · That Old Chestnut: complaints and institutional accountability

Turn observations into verified, fair process

That Old Chestnut is a discovery and comparison tool for wording about scope, searches, delay, refusal grounds and later disclosure. It is not a finding of motive or misconduct. The current linked Peekaboo feed contains six observations: TOC-F01 Museum Wales; TOC-F02 Historic England; TOC-F03 Vale of Glamorgan Council; TOC-F05 Welsh Government/Cadw; a Glamorgan Archives catalogue correction (DBDT/79/1); and a Newman/Internet Archive metadata mismatch. Recheck all six against complete original threads, later mail, attachments and actual records before deciding what complaint, if any, is justified.

Observation2027 forensic work
TOC-F01 · Museum WalesReconstruct initial not-held statement, later records, request scope, search steps, custody and any residual unanswered question; determine if internal review/ICO route remains available.
TOC-F02 · Historic EnglandCompare the 21 October reply and 27 October later-found record; establish record identity, search scope, dates and whether the original request was answered fully.
TOC-F03 · Vale of Glamorgan CouncilReconcile 2 June not-held statement with 11 September confirmation that further historical documentation was found; obtain the sifting/disclosure trail and final disposition.
TOC-F05 · Welsh Government/CadwCompare 25 February scope position with 21 May identification of 1988 assessment and two photographs; separately assess the Sept–Oct complaint handling and Ombudsman route.
OBS-PEEKABOO-DBDT79-CATALOGUEPreserve old and corrected catalogue descriptions; verify physical original and correction history; distinguish catalogue error from any broader claim.
OBS-PEEKABOO-NEWMAN-IACompare Internet Archive metadata and underlying item; document mismatch, provenance and any correction request.

Complaint workflow: (1) establish exact representation or omission; (2) identify the source and full chronology; (3) test whether later disclosure concerns the same record and scope; (4) identify applicable policy/statutory duty; (5) identify impact and remedy; (6) check internal route, time limit and jurisdiction; (7) draft neutral complaint; (8) independent review; (9) Executive authorisation; (10) file and track outcome. A complaint is not filed simply because an answer was late, unsatisfactory or inconsistent.

9 · Reporting, metrics and agent acceptance

Weekly, monthly and stage-end reporting
  • Daily: inbox triage, next-action exceptions, due-today and overdue list, unowned work, unread attachments, drafts waiting review.
  • Weekly: each institution's milestone/work-package/task progress; questions answered/unanswered; request clock performance; requester response latency; authority response latency; risks/issues/decisions; top five next actions.
  • Monthly highlight report: PRINCE2 status, products accepted, spend, stage forecast, tolerance breaches, complaints, lessons, dependencies and decisions needed.
  • Stage-end report: product acceptance, residual risks, business case, lessons and recommendation to proceed/amend/pause/stop.

Machine-readable programme register: programme.json. Each task has a stable ID, milestone, work package, description, owner role, reviewer role, status, priority, acceptance criteria and evidence requirements.

Completion definition: task output exists; sources are cited; exact question is answered or its disposition justified; contradictory evidence is recorded; independent QA passes; next action is set; status and report data are updated. “Email sent”, “reply received” and “agent ran search” are activities, not completed outcomes.

Prepared 25 November 2026 from the currently available Projects, Dartboard, Gmail and That Old Chestnut records. Draft programme baseline only; Q&A source discovery, year-end mailbox reconciliation and HR-07 completion remain explicit mobilisation tasks. No complaint or FOI is filed by publishing this plan.
